# Conformance sheet

Which parts of the [GraphQL spec](https://spec.graphql.org/September2026/), September 2026 (latest release), each implementation was checked against, and what it breaks. Every verdict comes from a report written by the [conformance kit](../docs/conformance.md), which drives the implementation through an adapter and checks each response against the TLA+ reference. Section numbers are from the September 2026 edition (spec/Section 2–7; graphql/graphql-spec@bfcc722b32f4, the working draft the kit was written from, numbers them the same). Regenerate with `npm run conformance`.

Read it with these limits in mind:

- **Small fixtures.** A ✓ means no case the kit tried broke the rule, not that the implementation is correct. The cell gives the number of cases it rests on.
- **One oracle.** The TLA+ references were checked against graphql-js only (differentially, and through the negative controls, which are graphql-js cases broken on purpose). A bug the reference shares with graphql-js would show as ✓ everywhere.
- **Unreviewed adapters.** The kit's authors wrote every adapter, and no implementation's maintainers have reviewed theirs yet. A failure may be the adapter's, not the implementation's. Each adapter's README says how it maps the protocol onto its implementation.
- **Edition.** The kit grades the September 2026 edition, the latest release: its rules and its levels. Where September 2025 or October 2021 gives a check another level, its level says so. Failing cases that rely on grammar or rules introduced after October 2021 are counted by the release that introduced them, "since September 2025" or "new in September 2026", so an implementation of an older edition can tell them apart.

**Finding groups** counts kinds of failure, not distinct bugs: one root cause can show up as several groups, and the number isn't a ranking. **Cases failing direct checks** are cases the kit rejected before consulting the reference, for something it checks on its own, such as a response stream that failed, a leaf value that should have raised an error, or a malformed error entry. They are the implementation's failures unless the adapter-problems column says otherwise.

## Findings

Each finding is one kind of failure: the set of clauses or direct checks a case broke. A case belongs to one finding, but a malformed case counts against every check it breaks, so one problem never hides another.

- **[graphql-js 16.15.0](graphql-js-16.md):** 
  - (SHOULD) [`nothingAfterCancel`](graphql-js-16.md#execution-finding-1): a result arrived after the client cancelled the response stream (4 cases, `subscription`, `subscriptionAlias`)
  - (new in September 2026) [`directiveExtension`](graphql-js-16.md#parse-finding-1): rejects what §3.13.6 Directive Extensions accepts (14 cases, `type-system-documents`, `grammar-examples`, `extensions`)
  - (new in September 2026) [`selectionSets`](graphql-js-16.md#parse-finding-2): rejects what §2.5 Selection Sets accepts (11 cases, `random-documents`, `parser-examples`, `type-system-documents`, `field-suffixes`, `grammar-examples`)
  - (new in September 2026) [`directiveDefinition`](graphql-js-16.md#parse-finding-3): rejects what §3.13 Directives accepts (5 cases, `directive-definitions`, `grammar-examples`)
  - (since September 2025) [`stringValue`](graphql-js-16.md#parse-finding-4): rejects what §2.10.4 String Value accepts (2 cases, `lexer-examples`, `strings`)
  - [`fieldSelectionMerging`](graphql-js-16.md#validate-finding-1): §5.3.2 Field Selection Merging isn't enforced: accepts documents that break only this rule (22 cases, `merging`)
  - [`singleRootField`](graphql-js-16.md#validate-finding-2): §5.2.4.1 Single Root Field isn't enforced: accepts documents that break only this rule; 6 of 17 rely on rules since September 2025 (17 cases, `operations`, `twins`, `random`)
  - (since September 2025) [`operationTypeExistence`](graphql-js-16.md#validate-finding-3): §5.2.1.1 Operation Type Existence isn't enforced: accepts documents that break only this rule; all rely on rules since September 2025 (15 cases, `operations`, `twins`, `spec`)
  - [`singleRootField`](graphql-js-16.md#validate-finding-4): crashes on documents that break only §5.2.4.1 Single Root Field, instead of rejecting them (11 cases, `operations`, `spec`, `extra`)
  - [`valuesOfCorrectType`](graphql-js-16.md#validate-finding-5): §5.6.1 Values of Correct Type isn't enforced: accepts documents that break only this rule (6 cases, `twins`)
  - (new in September 2026) [`executableDefinitions`](graphql-js-16.md#validate-finding-6): crashes on documents that break only §5.1.1 Executable Definitions, instead of rejecting them; all rely on rules since September 2026 (2 cases, `twins`)
  - (new in September 2026) [`leafFieldSelections`](graphql-js-16.md#validate-finding-7): crashes on documents that break only §5.3.3 Leaf Field Selections, instead of rejecting them; all rely on rules since September 2026 (2 cases, `twins`, `spec`)
  - (since September 2025) [`allVariableUsagesAreAllowed`](graphql-js-16.md#validate-finding-8): §5.8.5 All Variable Usages Are Allowed isn't enforced: accepts documents that break only this rule; all rely on rules since September 2025 (2 cases, `spec`)
  - (new in September 2026) [`acceptsValid`](graphql-js-16.md#validate-finding-9): crashes on valid documents instead of accepting them; hint: §5.3.3 Leaf Field Selections, the rules their invalid siblings break; all rely on rules since September 2026 (1 case, `spec`)
  - [`inputObject`](graphql-js-16.md#coerce-finding-1): accepts what §3.10 (Input Objects) rejects (5 cases, `literal`, `spec-example`)
  - [`notExecutable`](graphql-js-16.md#coerce-finding-2): accepts what §2.3 (Document) rejects (5 cases, `not-executable`)

- **[graphql-dotnet 8.8.5](graphql-dotnet.md):** 
  - (SHOULD) [`errorLocationsPresent`](graphql-dotnet.md#execution-finding-1): execution errors raised at a field have no locations (2690 cases, `mutationRoot`, `merging`, `ordering`, `fragments`, `abstract`)
  - [`dataPresent`](graphql-dotnet.md#execution-finding-2): the response has no data entry (1502 cases, `subscriptionAlias`)
  - [`errorPath`](graphql-dotnet.md#execution-finding-3): an execution error without a path (1502 cases, `subscriptionAlias`)
  - [`resultCoercion`](graphql-dotnet.md#execution-finding-4): the implementation produced no response, after a resolver returned a value its type can't represent: … threw ArgumentException: .NET number values such as positive and negative infinity cannot be written as valid JSON. To make it work when using 'JsonSerializer', consider specifying … (see … (365 cases, `leaves`)
  - [`eventPerSourceEvent`](graphql-dotnet.md#execution-finding-5): a result arrived for no source event (18 cases, `subscriptionAlias`)
  - [`completesWithSource`](graphql-dotnet.md#execution-finding-6): the source completed, and the response stream never completed (8 cases, `subscriptionAlias`)
  - [`cancelCompletes`](graphql-dotnet.md#execution-finding-7): the client cancelled the response stream, which then never completed (6 cases, `subscriptionAlias`)
  - [`cancelCancelsSource`](graphql-dotnet.md#execution-finding-8): the client cancelled the response stream, and the source stream wasn't cancelled (6 cases, `subscriptionAlias`)

## Execution: each check

- **✓ N:** every one of the N cases it applied to satisfied it.
- **✗ n/N:** n of N cases broke a MUST. **! n/N:** a SHOULD.
- **i n/N:** an informational check, which no spec sentence requires (for example graphql-js's experimental execution without error propagation, or how a JSON parser reads large numbers), differed in n of N cases. Never a failure.
- **(k since September 2025, j new in September 2026), (all …):** k of the n failing cases rely on grammar or rules introduced in September 2025, and j on ones introduced in September 2026; none of them is in the October 2021 edition. The symbol still gives the check's level.
- **~:** inconclusive (see the implementation's page).
- **⊂:** subsumed: every input that breaks this rule also breaks a broader one, so a rejection can't be credited to it (accepting such an input still fails it).
- **–:** never exercised: the adapter lacks the capability, or no case reached it. **– needs `x`:** the adapter doesn't claim capability `x`, which the check needs.
- **n/a:** the adapter doesn't take part in this area, or its report predates the check.

### Execution: each fixture

The sections a fixture lists are the ones it exercises; its verdict is whether any of its cases failed a check: ✗ n/N if a failing case broke a MUST, ! n/N if the failing cases broke only SHOULDs, where n counts every failing case; i n/N if they only differed on an informational check.

## Parse: each check

- **✓ N:** every one of the N cases it applied to satisfied it.
- **✗ n/N:** n of N cases broke a MUST. **! n/N:** a SHOULD.
- **i n/N:** an informational check, which no spec sentence requires (for example graphql-js's experimental execution without error propagation, or how a JSON parser reads large numbers), differed in n of N cases. Never a failure.
- **(k since September 2025, j new in September 2026), (all …):** k of the n failing cases rely on grammar or rules introduced in September 2025, and j on ones introduced in September 2026; none of them is in the October 2021 edition. The symbol still gives the check's level.
- **~:** inconclusive (see the implementation's page).
- **⊂:** subsumed: every input that breaks this rule also breaks a broader one, so a rejection can't be credited to it (accepting such an input still fails it).
- **–:** never exercised: the adapter lacks the capability, or no case reached it. **– needs `x`:** the adapter doesn't claim capability `x`, which the check needs.
- **n/a:** the adapter doesn't take part in this area, or its report predates the check.

## Validate: each check

- **✓ N:** every one of the N cases it applied to satisfied it.
- **✗ n/N:** n of N cases broke a MUST. **! n/N:** a SHOULD.
- **i n/N:** an informational check, which no spec sentence requires (for example graphql-js's experimental execution without error propagation, or how a JSON parser reads large numbers), differed in n of N cases. Never a failure.
- **(k since September 2025, j new in September 2026), (all …):** k of the n failing cases rely on grammar or rules introduced in September 2025, and j on ones introduced in September 2026; none of them is in the October 2021 edition. The symbol still gives the check's level.
- **~:** inconclusive (see the implementation's page).
- **⊂:** subsumed: every input that breaks this rule also breaks a broader one, so a rejection can't be credited to it (accepting such an input still fails it).
- **–:** never exercised: the adapter lacks the capability, or no case reached it. **– needs `x`:** the adapter doesn't claim capability `x`, which the check needs.
- **n/a:** the adapter doesn't take part in this area, or its report predates the check.

## Coerce: each check

- **✓ N:** every one of the N cases it applied to satisfied it.
- **✗ n/N:** n of N cases broke a MUST. **! n/N:** a SHOULD.
- **i n/N:** an informational check, which no spec sentence requires (for example graphql-js's experimental execution without error propagation, or how a JSON parser reads large numbers), differed in n of N cases. Never a failure.
- **(k since September 2025, j new in September 2026), (all …):** k of the n failing cases rely on grammar or rules introduced in September 2025, and j on ones introduced in September 2026; none of them is in the October 2021 edition. The symbol still gives the check's level.
- **~:** inconclusive (see the implementation's page).
- **⊂:** subsumed: every input that breaks this rule also breaks a broader one, so a rejection can't be credited to it (accepting such an input still fails it).
- **–:** never exercised: the adapter lacks the capability, or no case reached it. **– needs `x`:** the adapter doesn't claim capability `x`, which the check needs.
- **n/a:** the adapter doesn't take part in this area, or its report predates the check.

## Schema: each check

- **✓ N:** every one of the N cases it applied to satisfied it.
- **✗ n/N:** n of N cases broke a MUST. **! n/N:** a SHOULD.
- **i n/N:** an informational check, which no spec sentence requires (for example graphql-js's experimental execution without error propagation, or how a JSON parser reads large numbers), differed in n of N cases. Never a failure.
- **(k since September 2025, j new in September 2026), (all …):** k of the n failing cases rely on grammar or rules introduced in September 2025, and j on ones introduced in September 2026; none of them is in the October 2021 edition. The symbol still gives the check's level.
- **~:** inconclusive (see the implementation's page).
- **⊂:** subsumed: every input that breaks this rule also breaks a broader one, so a rejection can't be credited to it (accepting such an input still fails it).
- **–:** never exercised: the adapter lacks the capability, or no case reached it. **– needs `x`:** the adapter doesn't claim capability `x`, which the check needs.
- **n/a:** the adapter doesn't take part in this area, or its report predates the check.

## Introspect: each check

- **✓ N:** every one of the N cases it applied to satisfied it.
- **✗ n/N:** n of N cases broke a MUST. **! n/N:** a SHOULD.
- **i n/N:** an informational check, which no spec sentence requires (for example graphql-js's experimental execution without error propagation, or how a JSON parser reads large numbers), differed in n of N cases. Never a failure.
- **(k since September 2025, j new in September 2026), (all …):** k of the n failing cases rely on grammar or rules introduced in September 2025, and j on ones introduced in September 2026; none of them is in the October 2021 edition. The symbol still gives the check's level.
- **~:** inconclusive (see the implementation's page).
- **⊂:** subsumed: every input that breaks this rule also breaks a broader one, so a rejection can't be credited to it (accepting such an input still fails it).
- **–:** never exercised: the adapter lacks the capability, or no case reached it. **– needs `x`:** the adapter doesn't claim capability `x`, which the check needs.
- **n/a:** the adapter doesn't take part in this area, or its report predates the check.

## Coordinate: each check

- **✓ N:** every one of the N cases it applied to satisfied it.
- **✗ n/N:** n of N cases broke a MUST. **! n/N:** a SHOULD.
- **i n/N:** an informational check, which no spec sentence requires (for example graphql-js's experimental execution without error propagation, or how a JSON parser reads large numbers), differed in n of N cases. Never a failure.
- **(k since September 2025, j new in September 2026), (all …):** k of the n failing cases rely on grammar or rules introduced in September 2025, and j on ones introduced in September 2026; none of them is in the October 2021 edition. The symbol still gives the check's level.
- **~:** inconclusive (see the implementation's page).
- **⊂:** subsumed: every input that breaks this rule also breaks a broader one, so a rejection can't be credited to it (accepting such an input still fails it).
- **–:** never exercised: the adapter lacks the capability, or no case reached it. **– needs `x`:** the adapter doesn't claim capability `x`, which the check needs.
- **n/a:** the adapter doesn't take part in this area, or its report predates the check.

| No whitespace, line terminator, comma, comment or byte order mark anywhere (`noIgnored`) | MUST (since September 2025) | [§2.14](https://spec.graphql.org/September2026/#sec-Schema-Coordinates) | ✓ 25 | ✓ 25 | n/a | n/a | ✗ 23/25 (all since September 2025) | ✓ 25 | n/a | n/a | n/a | n/a | ✗ 25/25 (all since September 2025) |

## By spec section

A section's verdict comes from the checks that cite it, and names the ones that failed, worst level first: ✗ for a MUST, ! for a SHOULD, i for an informational check that differed. Within a level, **(since September 2025)** and **(new in September 2026)** mark the checks whose failing cases all rely on grammar or rules introduced after October 2021, by the first release they fail in. With no MUST or SHOULD failing, ✓ means every check that ran passed or only differed informationally, ~ that one was inconclusive and – that none was exercised. Sections that fixtures exercise but no check targets have no verdict of their own; their fixtures' verdicts are above.
